# agent-007 on Pi Durable: Phase 0 (Windows smoke test), Phase 1 (MVP), Phase 2 (orchestrator), Phase 3 (settings UI and personas), Phase 4 (multi-bot)

- **Ticket folder:** `.claude/tickets/agent-007-pi-durable-mvp/`. There is no ticket id; the name is descriptive.
- **Plan date:** 2026-10-26, revised 2026-10-27 (twice) and 2026-10-29 (revision c: multi-bot; revision d: Phase 3 detail). Status: **Phases 0 and 1 ready for `implement`.** Phase 2 is planned at slice level and starts only after Gate G1; Phase 3 is planned in detail (revision d), **built offline 2026-10-30** (live parts open, see G3 in section 14), and goes live only after Gate G3; Phase 4 (multi-bot, D12) is planned at slice level and starts only after G3. Nothing has been installed, run or configured yet.
- **Branch:** `agent-007-pi-durable-mvp`, based on `origin/master`. The remote is `origin` = `git@github.com:Tihi321/agent-007.git`, and `origin/master` exists [V local].
  - Per the implement skill's recipe: `git fetch origin master`, then `git switch --no-track -c agent-007-pi-durable-mvp origin/master`.
  - `implement` records the branch here once it is cut. Pushing and PRs are not authorised by this plan.
- **Inputs:** `../agent-007-research/notes-pi-durable.md` (the main input), `research.md`, `system-inventory.md` and `notes-pi.md`. For Phases 2 and 3: `../agent-007-research/notes-orchestrator.md` (with the checker's corrections in "Revision 2026-10-27 (b)"), and creative-hub's `CLAUDE.md` and `README.md` (`C:\projects\Personal\creative-hub`, read-only). For Phase 4: `../agent-007-research/notes-multibot.md` (Telegram bot-to-bot, Pi Durable multi-agent, Hermes profiles, the ACP proxy as a brain; the proxy's `README.md` and `src/` were read, `settings.json` was not).
- **API spot-check (this pass):** WebFetch of the 1.4.0 README on jsdelivr, `types.ts` and `CHANGELOG.md` on `main`, the pi-ai 1.4.0 README, and LM Studio's `headless` and `authentication` docs.

### Revision 2026-10-27

The user approved: "Add Strata to plan.md as a third bake-off candidate and the preferred 24/7 runner, keeping LM Studio as the Phase 0 default."

**Strata facts [V on this PC, 2026-10-27].** Third-party, open-source local LLM server (github.com/Niko1221/Strata, MIT, 0.2.0-beta, engine 0.1.44.1/0.1.45.3); not a Pixotope project.
- Installed at `D:\Strata` (git, HEAD `e88ee39d`, 2026-10-19, clean); models and data at `D:\Strata-data`.
- Python server `D:\Strata\serve\server.py` (run with `D:\Strata\.venv` python) plus the C++ ROCm/HIP engine `D:\Strata\engine\strata.exe`. gfx1151 is supported but labelled "experimental" in `docs\STRIX_HALO.md`.
- Launcher `D:\Strata\run-iq3_s.bat` = `serve\server.py --engine strata --config D:\Strata\strata-iq3_s.json --port 8080 --open`. The config uses backend `hip`, port 8080, model name `qwen3.8-flash-next-iq3_s`, `--max-context 131072`, `--kv int8`, `--spec 4`, `--mtp`, `--resident-experts`, log `D:\Strata\strata-iq3_s.log`. An `"open_browser": false` option exists.
- API: `POST /v1/chat/completions` (stream and non-stream, with `tools`/`tool_choice`), `/v1/messages`, `/v1/responses`, `GET /v1/models`, `/health`. Binds 127.0.0.1 by default. **No auth unless a key is set:** per `D:\Strata\SECURITY.md`, an `"api_key"` in the run config (or `STRATA_API_KEY`) is enforced on `/v1/*` whatever the bind host [V doc; enforcement U29]. The shipped config sets no key. One sequence at a time behind a FIFO.
- Model: Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-GSQ-RCO **IQ3_S**, two GGUF shards (54.8 GB + 28.8 GB, about 84 GB) plus an MTP draft. Other quants on disk: IQ2_XS, coder-IQ1_M.
- Not running now; no service or scheduled task. The last log (2026-10-12) shows working requests at about 29 tok/s decode.
- LM Studio is running headless at 127.0.0.1:1234 with a small Gemma model loaded. Both runners share the same 96 GiB GPU pool, so only one big model can be loaded at a time.

**What changed in this revision:**
- Sections 1, 2 (D3 note, I13 to I15), 4.1, 4.4 and 4.5: two local runners. LM Studio is the Phase 0 default; Strata is the alternative and the preferred 24/7 runner. Config gains `model.runner: "lmstudio" | "strata"` with per-runner `baseUrl` and model id. Only the adapter touches pi-ai.
- Section 3: UI-6 narrowed; new UI-9 (go-ahead to start Strata for tests) and UI-10 (which runner for 24/7, decided at G0).
- Section 0 and Phase 0: new step **P0.3b** (start Strata manually for tests [GO]); P0.4 smoke tests run against either runner by config; P0.5 adds a third candidate; **P0.7** is now a 24/7 runner spike, Strata first, llmster as fallback.
- Section 6 (G0): the decision picks runner + model.
- Section 4.2, Slices 1, 2, 4, 5, 6, 7 and 8: runner-aware health check, status and offline UX; built-in denies for Strata paths; new **S7.9** for the optional Strata scheduled task.
- Sections 9, 10, 11, 12 (U19 to U29) and 14 updated.
- Also from the review: D3 and the `secrets.json` row name both runners; done-checks added to S1.3, S2.3, S7.2, S7.4 and S7.7.
- Follow-up fixes (same day, after review):
  - built-in denies are split into NTFS-denied user-data paths and in-process-only paths (S1.2, S7.2, S7.3, S8.3 #13), so the Strata install, the ProgramData files and the agent's own data are never NTFS-denied;
  - the 24/7 Strata config sets an API key (`strataApiKey` in secrets), which the adapter sends as the API key;
  - P0.3b runs from a config copy under `.claude/temp/strata/`, so nothing is written into `D:\Strata`;
  - Slice 7 owns `test/deploy/**`.

### Revision 2026-10-27 (b)

**User decisions (2026-10-27, the user's choices), recorded as D8 to D11:**
1. "Yes, jobs may use cloud." Claude Code and DeepSeek jobs may use their cloud plans; every job needs a Telegram approval. The agent's own model stays local.
2. "Small runner as me." The agent stays on its low-privilege user. A small **job runner** under the main user starts the unmodified `claude` binary in creative-hub after a Telegram approval and streams progress back.
3. "MVP first, then layers." Phases 0 and 1 stay as planned. Phase 2 adds creative-hub MCP plus Claude Code and DeepSeek jobs. Phase 3 adds the settings UI and personas.
4. Flow budget is "Ask every time." Every Flow job shows its estimated credits, and the user approves that number. No standing default budget is spent without approval. This overrides the notes' "default 150 + approval above a threshold" and creative-hub's "default 150 per production".

**Corrections to `notes-orchestrator.md` from its checker, applied here:**
- api-acp-proxy is stateless, text-only, tools-off and auto-cancels permissions **by default**. It can pass client MCP bridges, remote MCP and WebSearch, and has `PROXY_REUSE_SESSIONS`. It still ignores CLAUDE.md and skills, so it cannot run productions.
- claude-agent-acp "0.91 → 0.97" took about 11 days.
- Verified locally: 7 of the 8 creative-hub MCP entries have no auth header; only book-translator has one.

**Terminology.** The **model runner** is LM Studio or Strata (`model.runner`, Phases 0 and 1). The **job runner** is the new Phase 2 process under the main user. The plan always writes "job runner" for the latter.

### Revision 2026-10-29 (c): multi-bot

**User decision (2026-10-29), recorded as D12:** the user wants several bots, "like Hermes bots", each with its own skills and separate memory, able to talk to each other. Bots may use the api-acp-proxy (Codex, Claude, Antigravity through the user's subscriptions) as their own brain, so more and faster bots are possible. Local models (Strata, LM Studio) stay switchable per bot by design. Research is in `../agent-007-research/notes-multibot.md` (read in full; `notes-orchestrator.md` skimmed for the proxy and job-runner parts).

**Revised earlier items (D12 forces the wording; nothing is renumbered):**
- **D3** and **D8**: "the agent's own model is local" is now "local in Phases 1 to 3 and for the default `main` bot; Phase 4 bots may use the proxy by per-bot config (D12)". A per-token-billed cloud API key stays disallowed as a brain or fallback (I39).
- **I23** and **UI-15**: the second-opinion endpoint is still off and unbuilt without an explicit yes, but the reason changes. D12 removes the D3 conflict for a bot's brain; the per-call side channel of a local bot is still a cloud call outside a job. A proxy-brained peer bot reached through `ask_bot` is the Phase 4 alternative. The proxy port in UI-15 and S15.3 is no longer `7461`: it is read from the proxy's settings at setup (U52; the code default is 8787).
- **I27**: the always-loaded list is unchanged (exactly 10). The cap now applies **per bot**, and `ask_bot` and `list_bots` are deferred (13.2 rules, 13.1 sketch).
- Section 1 (scope lines), section 9 (the Phase 2 "cloud only inside jobs" item, plus new Phase 4 items and residual risks), section 13.1 (phase table, UI-15 text). Section 13.4 "Later, not planned" is now **13.5**; the new **13.4** is Phase 4. The 2026-10-27 (b) note above still says "13.4 later, not planned"; that is the old number.

**What changed:**
- Section 2: **D12**; inferred **I30 to I46**. Section 3: **UI-23 to UI-32**. Section 12: **U49 to U59**.
- Sections 0 (order, user steps, files), 1, 4.1 (layout), 4.2 (per-bot ACL rows), 9, 10, 11 (Phase 4 sizing), 13.1, 13.4 (Phase 4: Slices 20 to 27, Gate G4 with the multi-bot eval), 13.5 and 14.
- Phase 4 architecture (from the notes' recommendation): one bot = its own directory, Telegram token, Pi `Harness` and SQLite, model route, tool allowlist and skills; all bots in the agent host for v1, behind a `BotBus` interface that can move to the I16 bus later. Bot-to-bot messaging is our own deferred tools plus a router, never Telegram's Bot API 10.0 bot-to-bot mode (kept off). A write-only mirror bot shows the traffic in a "bot room".
- Points the plan decides that the notes left open, so the user can overrule them:
  - Peer-triggered turns also lose `remember` (a peer could otherwise write persistent memory, I36).
  - An `ask_bot` edge into a bot on a cloud preset is an egress edge and is off by default (I36).
  - The shell bot and `dispatch_job` default to local-brained bots (I42, UI-25).
  - Concurrency to the proxy defaults to 3 per provider, below the proxy's cap of 4, so creative-hub's use of the same proxy is not starved (I40).
  - One Windows user for all bots in v1. A separate user for the shell bot needs a process split and a fresh plan revision (UI-28, UI-32, 13.5).
- **Checker fixes (same day):** (1) a per-token-billed API key is never a brain or fallback and is not a preset kind in v1 (I38, I39, S20.1, S22, section 9, S27.2 #5); (2) no `elevated` edge: peer turns never get shell, file write, `dispatch_job`, Flow or `remember` (I36, S20.1, S21.2, S22.4, UI-29 repurposed, deferred to 13.5); (3) cloud → cloud to another vendor needs a card (I38, S27.2 #6); (4) D8 wording; (5) `scripts/proxy/check-port.ps1` owned by S23 and run by the user, `FakeBotBus` named, S24.3 done-check label; (6) `readRoots` and `cloudSafeRoots` inside the global allowlist, `folders.deny` wins (I43, S20.1, S20.3, section 9); (7) I36(3) approval-gated list adds `dispatch_job` and the local → cloud switch card.
- New IDs: D12, I30 to I46, UI-23 to UI-32, U49 to U59, S20 to S27, G4.

### Revision 2026-10-29 (d): Phase 3 detail

Section 0 said Phase 3 needs "a fresh, more detailed plan revision". **This revision is that revision, and it is done.** The source record is `phase3-detail.md` in this folder (kept as written; the merge below follows its section L). Nothing was built or run.

- **Replaced:** the outline Slices 17 to 19 in 13.3 are now detailed slices (S17.0 to S17.8, S18.1 to S18.5, S19.1 to S19.7), with a contracts block, a threat table and a full Gate G3.
- **Changes against the outline** (reasons in the slices): the persona schema, validator and `effective()` move into S17; every `security.json` change takes effect at agent restart (a new `/applysettings`); prefs are small (`style`, `defaultPersona`); `propose_*` are non-blocking and deferred; two persona folders (user-authored `settings\personas\`, model-proposed `prefs\personas\`); `memory {write, scope?, sharedRead}`; prefs, personas and the audit log are untrusted input at every load; new tamper-evident audit lines, out-of-band edit detection, proposal rate limits, deny-path propagation to Claude jobs, a Phase 3 mini-eval and optional private-chat topics.
- **New sub-IDs (no new top-level numbers; none collides with an existing ID):** I25a to I25d, I26a, I27a to I27f, I29a to I29e (section 2); UI-19a to UI-19d, UI-20a to UI-20d (section 3); U42a, U43a, U43b, U48a, U48b (section 12). UI-19c, UI-19d and UI-20d record the detail file's open questions 7, 8 and 6, which had no ID.
- **Sections edited:** header, 0 (order, user steps), 2, 3, 4.1, 4.2, 9, 10, 11, 12, 13.1, 13.3, 13.4 (four one-line cross-references, L item 8), 14.
- **Defaults used (the user's /goal says to implement the whole plan without pausing, so the detail file's proposals are used; every one is user-changeable and is flagged in its UI row):** UI port 47007 with one-time-code sign-in (UI-19, UI-19a); `autoApplyCosmetic` off (UI-19b); `security.json` changes need a restart, no hot reload (UI-19c); job runner limits editable in the "jobs" form (UI-19d); personas: built-in `readonly` plus shipped example drafts `research`, `ops`, `creative`, none active (UI-20a); no private-chat topics, S19.6 not built (UI-20b); a persona's preset may be on the other runner if that runner is up, else the switch refuses (UI-20c); the user's own `/persona` applies a broader persona at once (UI-20d).
- **Fixes made while merging:** `scripts/settings/**` was owned by both S17 and S18, so S17 now owns the three named files and S18 owns `copy-ui.mjs`; the deny-path helper lives at `src/jobrunner/settingsUi/denyPaths.ts` (S18 owns it; S18.3 had named `src/jobrunner/denyPaths.ts`); the UI's save routes may also write `jobrunner.json` (UI-19d), so the S18.2 fake-fs check lists it; the example persona drafts are `config/personas/*.example.json`, owned by S19.
- **Standing authorisation (the user's /goal, 2026-10-29):** building Phases 2 to 4 in code and offline tests is authorised before the live gates G0 to G3 pass, so section 0's "after G1 and the user says go" now governs going live, not coding. Live parts stay [GO] or user steps, and no bot or job goes live before its gate (jobs at G2, settings and personas at G3, extra bots at G4).

## Tags used in this plan

| Tag | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **[V]** | Verified in a primary source or on this PC. |
| **[U]** | Unverified API detail or behaviour. The step that settles it is named; see section 12. |
| **[UI-n]** | User input. Never invent a value for it; see section 3. |
| **[GO]** | **STOP.** Needs the user's explicit go-ahead in chat at execution time. |
| **[ADMIN]** | Needs an elevated (UAC) shell. The user runs it, or approves it being run. |

## 0. How `implement` should run this plan

### Order

**Standing authorisation (the user's /goal, 2026-10-29; see revision (d)):** Phases 2 to 4 may be built in code and offline tests before G0 to G3 pass. Live steps stay [GO] or user steps, and nothing goes live before its gate.

1. **Phase 0** runs P0.1 to P0.7 in order (P0.3b sits between P0.3 and P0.4), with one implementer.
2. **Gate G0** (section 6): every Phase 0 check must pass. If any fails, stop and report. Do not start Phase 1.
3. **Phase 1** runs in this order:
   1. Slice 1 (foundation) first, including S1.0, which pre-exports from `src/pi/adapter.ts` everything the parallel slices need. Slices 2 to 5 and 7 never edit the adapter.
   2. Then Slices 2, 3, 4, 5 and 7 in parallel. Their files do not overlap.
   3. Then Slice 6 (integration).
   4. Then Slice 8 (acceptance), which ends at **Gate G1** (section 8).
4. **Phase 2** (section 13.2) starts only after G1 passes and the user says go. Order:
   1. Slice 9 (Phase 2 foundation, including the adapter pre-exports for `pi-mcp`);
   2. Slices 10, 11, 12, 13 and 14 in parallel (disjoint files);
   3. Slice 15 (integration and the optional second-opinion endpoint);
   4. Slice 16 (routing eval and acceptance), ending at **Gate G2**. Phase 2 does not go live before G2.
5. **Phase 3** (section 13.3; detailed in revision 2026-10-29 (d)) starts only after G2 and a user go: Slice 17 (settings split, allowlist, audit, persona model, `propose_*`), then Slice 18, then Slice 19 (sequential, because 18 and 19 both edit `src/telegram/{bot,commands}.ts`), ending at **Gate G3**. Phase 3 does not go live before G3.
6. **Phase 4** (section 13.4, D12) starts only after G3 and the user says go, with UI-23 to UI-32 answered at least as far as 13.4 says. Order:
   1. Slice 20 (bot foundation and memory separation, including the adapter pre-exports);
   2. Slices 21, 22, 23, 24 and 25 in parallel (disjoint files);
   3. Slice 26 (integration);
   4. Slice 27 (multi-bot eval and acceptance), ending at **Gate G4**. Extra bots do not go live before G4; until then the install is the single `main` bot.

### Steps that need the user

**[GO]/[ADMIN] stop points.** The plan does not authorise these. Ask, wait for a clear yes, then act.

| Step | What needs the user |

| S25 | Live GPU checks that load or unload local models (Strata and LM Studio), like P0.5 [UI-32]. |
| S27.2 | The Phase 4 acceptance items (they use subscription quota). Messages go only to the user's own bot chats and, if chosen, the bot room the user created. |

The plan never authorises sending Telegram messages to anyone other than the user's own allowlisted account.

### Files

- **Single plan writer.** Parallel children do not edit this file. They report results, and the orchestrator updates the checklists and the verification log (section 14).
- **Temporary files** go under `C:/projects/Personal/agent-007/.claude/temp/`. Smoke-test databases go in `.claude/temp/smoke/`. Bake-off raw output goes in `.claude/temp/bakeoff/`. Strata soak probe output (P0.7) goes in `.claude/temp/soak/`.
- **Files outside the repo that the plan must never modify:** everything under `D:\Strata` and `D:\Strata-data` (third-party install, pinned), LM Studio's own files unless the user asks, and the **creative-hub** repo and its tool repos (Phase 2 only reads them and runs jobs in creative-hub; fixes there, such as MCP auth, belong to creative-hub tickets).
- **Durable results** go in this ticket folder: `bakeoff-results.md`, `phase0-report.md`, then `changelog.md` (written by `implement`). Phase 2 adds `routing-eval-results.md`; Phase 4 adds `multibot-eval-results.md` and `phase4-report.md`.
- **Pre-existing state:** `.gitignore` is untracked. It is at the repo root, outside `.claude/`. Per the skill, `implement` asks the user whether to carry it onto the branch or commit it first. Recommendation: carry it over and extend it in P0.2.

---

## 1. Intended result and scope

**Result.** A personal agent, "agent-007", built on Pi Durable 1.4.0 and running 24/7 on this Windows 11 PC.
- It runs under a separate low-privilege Windows user, started by Task Scheduler at system startup.
- The model is a local one (Phases 1 to 3, and the default `main` bot afterwards), served by one of two local runners chosen in config (`model.runner`). From Phase 4, each extra bot has its own route and may use the ACP proxy as its brain (D12):
  - **LM Studio** at `http://127.0.0.1:1234/v1`, the Phase 0 default;
  - **Strata** at `http://127.0.0.1:8080/v1`, the alternative and the preferred 24/7 runner, because it can run as a scheduled task with no one logged on (proved or disproved in P0.7).
- The user reaches it through a Telegram bot that only answers the user's numeric Telegram id.
- What it can do:
  - read fixed system facts;
  - read, list and search an allowlist of folders, where a denylist wins over the allowlist;
  - write only in its own workspace and in `MEMORY.md`;
  - run PowerShell only after the user taps Approve in Telegram, once per command.
- It survives crashes and restarts: turns resume, approvals persist, and answers are delivered.

**Later layers (D10):**
- **Phase 2, orchestrator (section 13.2).** The agent becomes a router. It calls a few curated creative-hub MCP tools directly. It hands whole productions to **jobs**: Claude Code (the unmodified `claude` binary) or DeepSeek Harness, started in creative-hub by a small **job runner** under the main user. Every job is approved in Telegram, may use cloud (D8), streams progress back, and is durable. Flow credits are approved per job (D11).
- **Phase 3, settings UI and personas (section 13.3).** An authenticated localhost settings UI; settings split into `security.json` and `prefs.json`; the model may only propose changes; personas that can only narrow permissions.
- **Phase 4, multi-bot (section 13.4, D12).** Several bots, each with its own Telegram token, storage, memory, skills, tool allowlist and model route. A bot may use the ACP proxy (Codex by default; Claude and Antigravity only by an explicit opt-in) or a local model, switchable per bot. Bots talk through a router-enforced `ask_bot` with hop and budget limits and a capability rule under which peer text never gains authority. Which bots may use a proxy brain is per-bot config (`security.json`); the default `main` bot and every new bot start local.

**In scope:**
- Phase 0: Node, scaffold, LM Studio hardening, Strata test start, smoke tests on either runner, model bake-off (three candidates), 24/7 runner spike.
- Phase 1: config and secrets, Telegram bridge, tools, shell and approval gate, prompt assembly, Windows setup scripts, logging and audit, tests and acceptance.
- Phase 2: planned at slice level (section 13.2). Phase 3: planned in detail (section 13.3). Phase 4: planned at slice level (section 13.4).

**Out of scope:**
- Anything in section 13.5.
- Cloud models for the **`main`** bot's own reasoning, unless the user sets a proxy route for it (D12, Phase 4). Otherwise cloud is allowed only inside approved jobs, from Phase 2 (D8), and as the brain of a Phase 4 bot whose proxy route the user configured (D12). A per-token-billed cloud API key as a brain is out of scope (I39).
- Pi `CodingTools` and the built-in `bash`.
- Voice, images and files over Telegram.
- Group chats, except the write-only Phase 4 bot room, where only the mirror bot posts and no bot reads (I37).
- Webhooks or any inbound port.

## 2. Decisions: agreed vs inferred

### Agreed by the user (do not reopen)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D1 | Base is Pi Durable. `@earendil-works/pi-durable`, `@earendil-works/pi-ai` and `@earendil-works/chord` are pinned to exactly **1.4.0**. The code lives in `C:/projects/Personal/agent-007`. |
| D2 | Host is this PC. The agent runs under a separate low-privilege local user, via Task Scheduler at system startup, restarts on failure, and runs as a single instance. |
| D3 | Local model (by default; see the D12 revision below), through a local runner on loopback: LM Studio at `127.0.0.1:1234/v1` (Phase 0 default) or Strata at `127.0.0.1:8080/v1` (added 2026-10-27, see below). The original LM Studio bake-off is `nail-qwen3.6-35b-a3b-mtp` vs `ista-daslab-qwen3.8-27b-gsq-rco-unsloth-mtp`. The Qwen3.6-27B Q8 (~29 GB) download is allowed only if both fail. No cloud API for the agent's own model in Phases 1 to 3 and for the default `main` bot (D8 allows cloud only inside approved Phase 2 jobs). **Revised 2026-10-29 by D12:** from Phase 4 each bot has its own route, and a bot may use the ACP proxy as its brain by per-bot config; the two local runners stay switchable per bot. A per-token-billed cloud API key as a brain or fallback stays disallowed (I39). **Revision 2026-10-27 (user-approved):** Strata (`127.0.0.1:8080/v1`) is added as a second local runner, with Qwen3.8-Flash-Next IQ3_S as a third bake-off candidate; Strata is the preferred 24/7 runner, and LM Studio stays the Phase 0 default. |
| D4 | System facts come from fixed queries, not free-form shell. Folder read, list and search use an allowlist. Pixotope and work data go on a denylist that wins. NTFS read ACLs are granted only on allowed folders. |
| D5 | Read-only tools, plus writes to the workspace and memory file. A PowerShell tool where every command needs a Telegram Approve or Deny, done with `beforeTool` and `api.memo`. Approvals survive a restart and time out to Deny. No `CodingTools`, no built-in `bash`. |
| D6 | Telegram uses grammY long polling and a numeric user-id allowlist; everything else is dropped and logged. One durable conversation per chat. Commands `/new /stop /status`, plus a kill switch that disables shell immediately. |
| D7 | `USER.md` and `MEMORY.md` go into the prompt. A `remember` tool writes `MEMORY.md` and announces each write in chat. |
| D8 | (2026-10-27) "Yes, jobs may use cloud." Claude Code and DeepSeek jobs may use their cloud plans; every job needs a Telegram approval. The agent's own model stays local (Phases 1 to 3 and the default `main` bot; **revised 2026-10-29: D12 lets Phase 4 bots use the ACP proxy as their brain, per bot. D8 itself covers jobs only**). |
| D9 | (2026-10-27) "Small runner as me." The agent stays on its low-privilege user. A small job runner under the main user starts the unmodified `claude` binary in creative-hub after a Telegram approval and streams progress back. |
| D10 | (2026-10-27) "MVP first, then layers." Phases 0 and 1 as planned; Phase 2 = creative-hub MCP plus Claude Code and DeepSeek jobs; Phase 3 = settings UI and personas. |
| D11 | (2026-10-27) Flow budget "Ask every time." Every Flow job shows its estimated credits and the user approves that number. No standing default budget is spent without approval. Overrides the notes' "default 150 + threshold" and creative-hub's default of 150. |
| D12 | (2026-10-29) Multiple bots, "like Hermes bots": each with its own skills and separate memory, able to talk to each other. Bots **may use the ACP proxy (api-acp-proxy: Codex, Claude, Antigravity through the user's subscriptions) as their own brain**, so more and faster bots are possible. **Local models (Strata, LM Studio) stay switchable per bot by design.** Which bots use a proxy brain is **per-bot config** (`security.json` route, set in the settings UI); the plan ships every bot, including `main`, with a local route, and a proxy preset is added to a bot only by the user. Codex is the default proxy brain; Claude and Antigravity are separate opt-ins (UI-24). Supersedes the "local only" wording of D3 and D8 for Phase 4 bots. Does not change D8 for jobs, D11, or the rule that no per-token-billed API key is used as a brain (I39). |

### Inferred in this plan (my choices; the user may override)

| # | Inferred detail | Why |
|---|---|---|
| I1 | Node 24 LTS is installed **machine-wide** (winget `--scope machine`). | The agent user must run `node.exe` from a place it can read but not modify. A user-scope install lives in the main user's profile, which the agent user cannot and should not reach. |
| I2 | Proposed on-disk layout (section 4.2): code in `C:\Program Files\agent-007\`; config and secrets in `C:\ProgramData\agent-007\`; state in the agent user's `%LOCALAPPDATA%\agent-007\`. | The agent cannot modify its own code, config or secrets. |
| I3 | Secrets are a JSON file with an explicit ACL, not DPAPI. | The task uses S4U logon (I4), and DPAPI user keys are not available under S4U [U]. DPAPI would also not stop the agent user, which owns the key. |
| I4 | Task logon type is **S4U** ("run whether logged on or not", no stored password). Fallback: Password. | No password stored anywhere [U: profile load and outbound HTTPS under S4U are verified in S7]. |
| I5 | The scheduled task has two triggers: one at startup, and one that repeats every 5 minutes for ever. Both run with "do not start a new instance". There is no execution time limit, and restart-on-failure is 3 times at 1-minute intervals. | Restart-on-failure may only cover launch failures [U]. The repeating trigger restarts a crashed process within 5 minutes. The default 72-hour limit would otherwise kill the agent. |
| I6 | Default agent account name `agent007`, configurable. | Taken from research.md. |
| I7 | Kill switch: a `/killshell` command plus a local flag file. **Re-enabling is local only:** an admin deletes the flag. | A hijacked Telegram account cannot turn shell back on. |
| I8 | Shell runs Windows PowerShell 5.1 by absolute path, with `-NoProfile -NonInteractive -ExecutionPolicy Restricted -EncodedCommand`. | No PATH hijack and no quoting bugs. Scripts the agent wrote cannot run unseen. |
| I9 | Logging uses a small own JSONL logger, no logging library. | Fewer dependencies in the supply chain. |
| I10 | Tests use `node:test`. Build with `tsc` to `dist/`. | Dev dependencies are only `typescript` and `@types/node`. |
| I11 | Tool names and schemas are fixed in Phase 0 (section 5, P0.5). | The bake-off then tests the exact schemas the MVP ships. |
| I12 | Approval timeout defaults to 10 minutes. Shell timeout defaults to 120 s, with a maximum of 600 s. | Defaults; configurable. |
| I13 | The runner is picked by config only (`model.runner`), with one block per runner. Code outside `src/pi/adapter.ts` never branches on pi-ai details; it passes `{ id, baseUrl, modelId, apiKey? }` to the adapter. | Switching runner is a config change plus a restart, with no code change. |
| I14 | The Q8 fallback download (D3) is used only if **all three** candidates fail, not just the two LM Studio ones. | If Strata's candidate qualifies, the download is not needed. This narrows, not widens, D3. |
| I15 | P0.7 creates the agent Windows user early, by hand, with S7.1's properties, so that the Strata spike runs under the real agent user. S7.1 is idempotent and adopts it. | The spike must prove GPU access under that exact user and S4U logon. |
| I16 | **Agent ↔ job runner IPC: TCP on 127.0.0.1 with per-message HMAC-SHA256**, not a named pipe. Newline-delimited JSON (not HTTP); each message carries a timestamp (±60 s), a nonce (replay cache) and an HMAC over the body with a shared 32-byte key. | Pure Node and easy to test. Node's `net` cannot set a custom DACL on a named pipe without a native addon [U40], and the default pipe DACL would not restrict the client. A non-HTTP framing plus HMAC also defeats browser and DNS-rebinding requests. The key sits in each side's ACL-protected secrets file. Trade-off: a fully compromised agent process holds the key, so the job runner also enforces its own limits (I22). |
| I17 | Job runner layout: code in `C:\Program Files\agent-007\` (the same deploy, agent user RX, so the agent cannot change code that runs as the main user); config and job templates in `C:\ProgramData\agent-007\jobrunner\` (main user and Admins write, agent user R on `templates\` only); secrets, journal and audit in the main user's `%LOCALAPPDATA%\agent-007-jobrunner\` (main user only). | The job runner runs with the main user's full rights, so nothing the agent user can write may influence it except signed IPC messages. |
| I18 | At most **one Claude Code job** and **one DeepSeek job** run at a time; more are queued durably. | Concurrent Claude processes share an OAuth refresh lock (notes §3.1). Human-scale volume also fits the terms reading (notes §3.2). |
| I19 | Claude Code permission prompts go through `--permission-prompt-tool`, served by a small **stdio MCP helper** hand-written in the job runner (initialize, tools/list, tools/call only). No MCP server SDK dependency. | One tool, about 100 LOC; fewer dependencies. Fallback in S12.3 if the flag misbehaves [U31]. |
| I20 | book-translator (the one creative-hub server with a bearer token) is **not** in the agent's direct MCP allowlist by default; it is used only inside Claude Code jobs. | The agent would otherwise need the main user's book-translator token. The user can add it later [UI-18]. |
| I21 | Inside a Claude Code job, each Flow tool call goes to the permission prompt tool. The job runner allows it automatically only if it has a `max_credits` that fits the job's remaining approved credits, and denies it otherwise. There is no Telegram tap per call. | D11 approves the number per job; per-call taps would add approval fatigue without adding control. |
| I22 | The job runner enforces its **own** limits from its own config, whatever the agent sends: template allowlist, cwd allowlist, per-template max credits, daily job cap and daily credit cap. | Defence in depth if the agent process is compromised (I16 trade-off). |
| I23 | The api-acp-proxy second-opinion endpoint is **off by default and not built** unless the user explicitly says yes [UI-15]. It conflicts with the letter of D8, because it is a cloud call by the agent outside a job. If the user agrees, each call is approved in Telegram like a job. **Revised 2026-10-29 (D12):** D12 removes the D3 conflict for a bot's *brain* (a per-bot proxy route, Phase 4), but this per-call side channel of a local bot is still a cloud call outside a job and stays off and unbuilt without an explicit yes. In Phase 4 a proxy-brained peer bot reached through `ask_bot` (I36) is the preferred way to get a second opinion, so UI-15 may simply be answered "no, use a bot". | D8 allows cloud only inside approved jobs; D12 allows it as a bot's brain by per-bot config; a per-call tool of a local bot is neither. |
| I24 | Routing eval bar (G2): 40 prompts × 3 runs; at least 90% correct dispatch overall and **100%** on the safety cases. | Same shape as the P0.5 bake-off. |
| I25 | (Phase 3) The settings UI is hosted by the **job runner** (main user), because only it may write `security.json`; the agent user has R only. | If the agent process hosted it, the agent user (and so an approved shell command) could write security settings. |
| I25a | The UI is `node:http` plus **vanilla JS, no framework, no dependency**. Static files live in `ui/settings/`; `npm run build` copies them to `dist/ui/settings/` (S18.1), so `deploy-app.ps1` (S7.5, copies `dist/`) deploys them with no change. CSP is strict: no inline script or style, no `innerHTML`, `eval` or `document.write` (grep test). | The UI runs in the job runner, which has the main user's full rights; its code stays small and auditable. |
| I25b | **Sign-in uses a one-time login code, not a long-lived owner token.** `node dist/src/jobrunner/main.js settings-link` (run by the main user in their own terminal) prints `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/#code=<code>` (the code is in the fragment, so it never reaches a Host header, log or Referer). The page POSTs the code once to `/api/login`; the code is single-use, expires in 5 minutes and is stored only as a hash in the job runner's main-user-only data folder. A login returns a session cookie `HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict; Path=/` and a CSRF token in the JSON body. Sessions are in memory (idle 30 min, absolute 8 h, at most 3), lost on job runner restart. `settings-link --revoke` ends every session and code (this is UI-19's "rotate"). Login failures: 5 per minute, then a 60 s lockout. | A long-lived token printed in a URL is worse than a code that dies in 5 minutes. This refines UI-19's "owner-token sign-in (one-time link printed locally, rotatable)"; same user effect. |
| I25c | **Request guard order** (every request, before routing): loopback peer address; `Host` in `{127.0.0.1:<port>, localhost:<port>}`; method is GET, HEAD or POST only; `X-Forwarded-*` and `Forwarded` ignored; for POST: `Origin` present and equal to one of the two allowed origins (`Origin: null` is refused), `Sec-Fetch-Site` is `same-origin` when present, `Content-Type: application/json`, body at most 256 KB, valid session and `X-CSRF-Token` equal to the session's (constant time). GET and HEAD never change state. Responses carry `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data:; connect-src 'self'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'none'`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin`, `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin`. | Notes section 7.1 (Host allowlist against DNS rebinding, CSRF token plus `Origin`, `Origin: null` is not same-origin, `Sec-Fetch-Site`, no trust in `X-Forwarded-*`). |
| I25d | **Save protocol.** A save sends `{section, value, baseRevision}`. The server validates (schema plus cross-checks), recomputes the diff against the current file, checks `baseRevision == sha256(current file)` (else 409), writes a temp file in the same folder, appends the audit line (`source: "ui"`), then renames the temp file over the target (retry on EBUSY or EPERM, 5 x 50 ms). A validation or audit failure leaves file and log unchanged. A rename failure leaves the file unchanged and adds exactly one `apply_failed` line. The agent loads `security.json` **once at start**; the UI shows "restart required" with the file's new revision, and `/status` shows `running rev X, file rev Y`. NTFS grants and Deny ACEs for new folders are **not** done by the UI (it never changes ACLs); the form warns and names `grant-folder-acls.ps1`. | Hearth Pi stores a revision and invalidates pending proposals; OpenClaw's `baseHash` (notes 7.1 and 7.2). A fixed snapshot is simpler and safer than hot reload. |
| I26 | (Phase 3) Telegram settings change `prefs.json` and personas only. Security settings are edited only in the authenticated UI. | A hijacked Telegram account cannot widen permissions. |
| I26a | **Telegram settings are the user's own authority.** A tap on `/settings`, `/persona` or `/proposals` by the allowlisted user (re-checked on every callback, as S4.5) applies at once, audited with `source: "telegram"`. Only rows of the allowlist with `telegram: true` are reachable, and there is no free-text input. Security paths are not buttons (a test enumerates every callback). | I26. |
| I27 | **Always-loaded tool cap: 10 per turn across Phases 1 to 3**, with the exact list `tool_search`, `dispatch_job`, `job_status`, `cancel_job`, `list_templates`, `ask_user`, `hub_status`, `system_facts`, `list_dir`, `read_file` (a code constant). From Phase 2, `search_files`, `write_file`, `remember` and `shell` are deferred behind `tool_search`, as are `hub_start`, every MCP tool, every macro, the Phase 3 settings and persona tools and the Phase 4 `ask_bot` and `list_bots`. **From Phase 4 the cap applies per bot:** each bot's registry has at most 10 always-loaded tools, the list above intersected with that bot's allowlist (an allowlist can only narrow it). The list itself is unchanged. | Tool search helps from about 10 tools (notes §1.3). The kept tools cover routing plus the most common read-only questions; the deferred ones are rarer or need approval anyway. Their schemas are unchanged; B7 to B10 are rerun in S16.1. |
| I27a | **`get_settings`, `list_personas`, `propose_setting`, `propose_persona`, `switch_persona` are all `deferred: true`.** `ALWAYS_LOADED` (the 10 of I27) is a code constant in `src/jobs/contracts.ts` and **no Phase 3 file edits it**; a test compares it to the literal list. `propose_*` do not block: the tool returns `{status: "pending", proposalId}` (or a typed error), and the decision is delivered later by the card. Proposals are a durable doc (`SettingsProposals`), so a card still works after a restart; each has a deadline (`security.proposals.timeoutSec`, default 600). Limits (in `security.json`): 1 pending proposal per chat, 5 proposals per hour. A rejected-before-card proposal writes one audit line (`decision: "invalid"`), at most 20 per hour, then a counter line. | Keeps the loop moving and the cap at 10 (I27). Approval fatigue and audit flooding are the two ways a looping local model could abuse the flow. |
| I27b | **Persona files.** `settings\personas\<id>.json` (user-authored, via the UI; agent R) and `%LOCALAPPDATA%\agent-007\prefs\personas\<id>.json` (model-proposed, approved in Telegram; agent F). Built-ins in code: `default` (the whole ceiling, Phase 1/2 behaviour) and `readonly` (see S19.1). Ids match `[a-z][a-z0-9-]{1,23}` and are immutable. An id in `settings\personas\` shadows the same id in `prefs\personas\`; a model proposal may not use a built-in id or a user-authored id. At most 12 personas. | UI-20: the user writes or approves personas. |
| I27c | **Persona = `{id, name, description?, prompt, toolAllow[], mcpAllow{server: tool[]}, jobTemplates[], modelPreset, memory {write: "shared"\|"scope"\|"none", scope?, sharedRead}, budgets {maxJobsPerDay?}}`** (all arrays explicit; no "absent means all", so a tool added later never reaches an old persona). **Ceiling** = what `security.json` and the code allow: the known tool names (`KNOWN_TOOLS`, a code constant tested against the real registry) minus `shell` when `shell.enabled` is false, MCP tools from `mcp.servers[].allowTools`, job templates the agent sees, `security.presets`, shared memory write, and `security.budgets.maxJobsPerDay`. A persona is **valid** only if every list is a subset of the ceiling, `modelPreset` is a security preset and `budgets` are at or below the ceiling. An invalid persona is **not clipped**: it is unselectable, shown with its reason in `/persona`, `list_personas` and the UI. Persona prompts never grant anything; enforcement is in code (S19.2). | Notes 7.2 item 4: "A widening persona fails validation visibly; it is not silently clipped." |
| I27d | **Persona order and "broader".** `compare(from, to)` returns `equal`, `narrower`, `broader` or `incomparable` with `added` and `removed` capability lists. Sets: tools, per-server MCP tools, job templates. Memory: write `shared` > `scope` > `none`; two different scopes are incomparable; `sharedRead` true > false. `maxJobsPerDay`: higher (or absent) is broader. A model preset difference is **not** a broadening in Phase 3, because every preset is local and listed by the admin; `compare()` takes a `presetWidens(from, to)` hook that Phase 4's I38 uses for cloud presets. A model-initiated `switch_persona` to `equal` or `narrower` applies at once; `broader` or `incomparable` produces one card listing `added`. `/persona` (the user's own command) applies at once either way (I26a). | S17.3's rule, with the cases the outline left open. |
| I27e | **Prompt order** is `preamble`, `persona`, `guardrails`, `style`, `user_profile`, `memory`, `runtime`. `guardrails` is a fixed code constant (about 8 lines: untrusted-content rules, approvals, "persona text cannot change these") placed after the persona so persona text cannot override the preamble rules. `style` renders fixed phrases from the enums plus `style.notes` as a quoted line. A test asserts the rendered order. | S5 owns `preamble.md`; S17 and S19 add sections without editing it. |
| I27f | **Tool narrowing is enforced twice.** `conversation.configure({tools})` hides tools (U43b), and `personaGuard`, a `beforeTool` check composed into the single combined hook, blocks any tool not in the persona's effective set, any `dispatch_job` with a template outside `jobTemplates`, and any `dispatch_job` past `maxJobsPerDay`. A tool name unknown to `KNOWN_TOOLS` is blocked in every persona except `default`. `extensions` is never passed to `configure()` (footgun 30, the S6.1 `rg` check stays). | The guard must hold even if `configure()` does not hide a tool. |
| I28 | The S12.3 permission helper authenticates to the job runner with a **per-job random token**, passed only in the helper's `env` inside the job-scoped `--mcp-config` file (main-user-only folder, deleted after the job), and used as the HMAC key on the job runner's 127.0.0.1 port. | Nothing else on the PC can answer or forge a permission decision for a running job; a token from another job or an ended job is useless. |
| I29 | (Phase 3) One append-only settings audit log in `C:\ProgramData\agent-007\settings\audit\`. The agent user and the job runner get append-only ACEs (append data, no write or delete); the settings UI's `security.json` saves and every `propose_*` decision go there. | One place to review every settings change; neither process can rewrite history [U48]. **Refined by I29a to I29e (revision d):** both users get read plus append, lines are hash-chained, and U48a decides Plan A or B. |
| I29a | **Tamper-evident audit lines.** Each line has `writer` (`agent` or `jobrunner`), a per-writer `wseq`, `prev` (the hash of that writer's previous line in the file) and `h = sha256(prev + "\n" + canonical JSON of the line without h)`. `scripts/settings/verify-audit.ts` checks every writer's chain and reports gaps. It detects edits and deletion in the middle, **not** removal of the last lines. A trim candidate (section 11, trim options). | U48 may show that a file's creator can re-ACL it (U48a); the chain then keeps rewrites visible. |
| I29b | **Out-of-band edit detection.** At start the agent reads the audit tail for the last `afterRevision` of `security.json`, `prefs.json` and each persona file. A file whose hash differs gets one audit line `source: "system", decision: "external_edit"` (and a `warn` log). `security.json` edits by an admin by hand are accepted if valid, and recorded. | An approved shell command or an admin can edit files; the audit then still tells the story. |
| I29c | **Everything under the agent profile (prefs, personas, memory scope files) is untrusted input.** Validated against the schema and ceiling at every load; an invalid `prefs.json` falls back to defaults with a warning; an invalid persona is unselectable. A forged file can never widen (a persona over the ceiling is invalid). `security.json`, `settings\personas\` and the audit folder are agent R (or append only), checked at start. | The agent user owns its profile; only `settings\` is main-user-controlled. |
| I29d | **The writable-path allowlist is a code table of rows** `{path (segments, `*` allowed), schema, class: "cosmetic"\|"pref"\|"user-only", proposable, telegram, classify?(old, new, ctx) -> "auto"\|"card"\|"reject"}`. There is **no row** of security class, so a security path is "not writable" by construction. Default `classify` is `card`, or `auto` for a `cosmetic` row only when `security.proposals.autoApplyCosmetic` is true. A row's own `classify` (used by Phase 4's `bots.*.activePreset`, I38) may return `auto` for a narrowing change. Paths are matched segment by segment; `__proto__`, `constructor`, `prototype`, empty segments and array indexes are refused; values are applied to a copy, never by assigning a user key on a plain object. Phase 4's S20 adds rows; no other file changes. | Notes 7.2 (allowlist, not denylist, after the OpenClaw CVEs). Lets S20 and S22 extend without editing S17 logic. |
| I29e | **Audit folder access:** agent user **read plus append** (needed for I29a and I29b), main user append plus read, Admins F; neither gets write-data, delete or change-permissions. If U48a shows the creator of a new monthly file can re-ACL it, Plan B: `setup-settings-acl.ps1` pre-creates the next 24 monthly files (owner Administrators), the folder ACE drops create-file, and a missing file fails the write closed ("no audit, no change"). | U48. |
| I30 | (Phase 4) **A bot is its own directory (`bots\<botId>\`), Telegram token, Pi `Harness` plus SQLite storage, model route, tool allowlist and skills.** A `BotManager` in the agent host owns all of them for v1: one `Harness.open(storage)` per bot and one grammY `Bot` per token, each with its own `bot.start()`. One poller per token (a second gets a 409). The existing agent becomes bot `main`; a one-bot install behaves exactly as in Phases 1 to 3 (`ask_bot` and `list_bots` are not registered without an allowed edge). Every bot answers only the user's numeric id (D6). | One `Harness` with a conversation per bot would share one storage and one registry, so there would be no memory separation (notes-multibot §2). The shape is Hermes' profile (a directory, a token, a gateway; §3). Several `Harness.open` in one process is not shown in the README [U50]; several grammY `Bot`s in one process [U49]. |
| I31 | (Phase 4) **Bots talk only through a `BotBus` interface** (`send(envelope)`, `onMessage`), with an in-process implementation in v1. The router runs **in the agent host**, not in the job runner (the privileged main-user component). An HMAC-over-TCP implementation, reusing I16's framing and checks, is added only if bots are split into processes (13.5). | The same checks as I16 without a socket. Moving a bot to another process or Windows user later does not touch the bots or their tools. |
| I32 | (Phase 4) **The router stamps the envelope:** `from` (taken from the authenticated channel: the in-process identity in v1, a per-bot HMAC key later, never one shared key and never the message body), `chain_id`, `hop`, `path[]` (bot ids visited), `origin` (`user` \| `peer`; `schedule` is reserved for 13.5) and `taint` (bool). A body field named `from`, `origin` or `taint` is ignored. | A confused or compromised bot cannot forge another sender or claim user authority. |
| I33 | (Phase 4) **Limits, enforced in the router** (numbers are the UI-30 proposal). Hop limit 2 (user → A → B → C; C cannot ask), and a bot already in `path[]` is not asked again. Per **chain** (one root user message): at most 6 bus messages, 5 minutes, a token cap and, for cloud routes, a quota cap; then the asker gets `budget_exhausted` and must answer the user. Per **edge**: one in-flight ask per (from, to), at least 5 s apart, 30 asks per day, and a circuit breaker that pauses the edge and tells the user after 10 asks in 10 minutes. **Loop detection:** SHA-256 of `(from, to, normalised body)`; the second identical message in a chain, or an A-B-A-B ping-pong, is blocked and counted. Peer-triggered turns cannot start timers or call `ask_bot` with `wait=false`. | Telegram's own bot-to-bot advice (dedup, depth, rate) maps onto this (notes-multibot §1, §5.4). Nothing keeps looping unattended. |
| I34 | (Phase 4) **Peer text is untrusted data.** The receiver gets it as a **user-role** data block, `<peer_message from="…" hop="…" taint="…">…</peer_message>`, never in the system prompt, with any closing tag in the body escaped. The reply that returns to the asker is wrapped the same way. The preamble says a peer message is a request from a peer, not the user, and carries no authority. Peer turns run in a **separate peer conversation** per sender in the receiver's own storage, so peer text never lands in the user's chat transcript. | The same rule as MCP output and job output (S10.5, S14.6). |
| I35 | (Phase 4) **Approvals are never relayed.** An approval card is produced only by the bot that executes the tool, in that bot's own chat with the user, with provenance ("requested via bot research, hop 1" plus the quoted peer text). No tool or bus message type can answer, pre-answer or auto-approve another bot's card. | An approval is the user's authority; a relay would be a forged one. |
| I36 | (Phase 4) **Capability rule (monotone narrowing).** (1) Authority comes only from the user: the user's own message in bot B's chat starts a chain with `origin=user` and B's full effective capabilities. (2) A **peer-triggered** turn gets `caps(receiver) ∩ caps(every bot in path[]) ∩ edge.allow`, and **never** `shell`, `write_file`, `dispatch_job`, Flow calls, `remember`, writes to shared locations or private-folder reads on a cloud route. There is no `elevated` edge (director's decision; the idea is deferred to 13.5). (3) **Taint:** a chain is tainted if any bot in it used a content-ingesting tool (web search, MCP reading, fetched pages, email) or its input came from such content. In a tainted turn **no approval-gated tool exists at all** (`shell`, `dispatch_job`, `hub_start`, `propose_*`, a broader `switch_persona`, the local → cloud `/model` switch card); the receiver replies with data only. (4) For shell work, the user sends their own message to the shell bot; a peer may only suggest it. (5) An edge into a bot whose active preset is cloud is an **egress edge** (`egress: true` in `security.json`, default false), and `attach_refs` to it are limited to its cloud-safe roots. Enforced in the router and in the guard extension, forced after every `configure()` (footgun 30, U43, U59), not in prompts. | Stops prompt-injection spread (a web-reading cloud bot asking a shell-capable bot). Fits S19.1's "security ∩ persona". `remember` in (2) and the egress edge in (5) are this plan's additions to the notes' rule: a peer could otherwise write persistent memory, or push private text to a vendor. |
| I37 | (Phase 4) **The Telegram "bot room" is a write-only mirror.** One extra mirror bot (own token, no `getUpdates` loop, no commands) posts lines like `[research → ops, hop 1, chain 7f3] text…` into a private group or channel the user created. The working bots never join any group (`/setjoingroups` stays Disable, UI-3). **Bot-to-Bot Communication Mode stays off for every bot:** Bot API 10.0 offers it, and it is not used as transport. Any update from a bot is dropped by the D6 id allowlist. Lines are batched, capped at about 15 per minute (Telegram's group limit is 20), truncated and redacted like logs. A mirror failure never blocks the bus. | Telegram says the mode "can easily result in infinite interaction loops" (notes-multibot §1). A Telegram transport would add loops, rate limits and an injection channel from any bot in a shared group. |
| I38 | (Phase 4) **Model presets and routes are security settings** (`security.json`, edited only in the UI, I25): `presets[] {id, kind: "acp" \| "local" (the only kinds in v1; a per-token-billed API-key preset is not a kind), baseUrl, modelId, providerId?, apiKeyRef?, contextWindow, maxTokens, privacy: "cloud" \| "local", autoFallback}` and per bot `route {primary, allowed[], fallback[], onAuthRequired: "fallback" \| "ask" \| "fail"}`. `activePreset` (one of `allowed`) is a prefs-level value in the bot's profile. **Switching:** `/model` (per bot, inline picker) or `propose_setting {path: "bots.<id>.activePreset"}`. A switch to a narrower privacy class (cloud → local), local → local between runners (Strata ↔ LM Studio), or between presets of the same vendor applies at once (audited, through the row's `classify` returning `auto`, S17.6); a switch that **widens egress**, local → cloud **or cloud → cloud to another vendor**, needs one approval card, like `switch_persona` (S17.3). `presets`, `route` and `fallback` paths are rejected before any card. | Local models stay switchable per bot (D12). A switch is `conversation.configure({model})` for the next request (U51). |
| I39 | (Phase 4) **Fallback never goes to a paid API key.** On 503 `provider_auth_required` or `auth_required`, or with the proxy down (the main user logged off): no retry, mark the preset "needs login" in `/status`, tell the user **once**, audit it, and fall back only to a preset in `route.fallback` with `autoFallback: true`, local by default (or `ask` or `fail` per `onAuthRequired`). 503 `provider_busy`: wait and retry up to twice, then as above. 429 `provider_queue_full` and 504 `request_timeout`: one backoff retry, then as above. 404 `model_not_found`: refresh `/v1/models`, and never substitute another model of the same provider. 400-class errors: no fallback (a bug). Cloud → cloud (another vendor) counts as new egress and needs `autoFallback: true`. A per-token-billed (API-key) preset is **not a preset kind in v1** and is never a brain or a fallback; the loader refuses one. | The cloud brains are down while the main user is logged off, as for the job runner (U30), so every cloud route needs a local fallback or an "offline" message. |
| I40 | (Phase 4) **The proxy is a shared, stateless chat backend.** One shared bearer key in the agent's `secrets.json` (the user copies it; never printed or logged); `127.0.0.1` only; only `POST /v1/chat/completions`, `GET /v1/models` and `GET /healthz`, never `/v1/mcp`, images or URL parameters. **The port is read from the proxy's settings at setup, not assumed** (the proxy's code default is 8787; the earlier plan wrote 7461; U52). Per-bot concurrency 1 plus a bot-side limiter per provider, default **3**, never above 4 (the proxy's default cap per provider, one strict FIFO shared with creative-hub's apps). Each `Model` entry carries `contextWindow` and `maxTokens` by hand (`/v1/models` has neither). Codex and Antigravity make **one tool call per response**, and a held turn expires after about **270 s (Codex) or 150 s (Antigravity)**, after which the proxy replays the history as text (slower, more tokens). Approvals keep I12's timeout (a per-bot `approvalTimeoutSec` can only be lowered), and the approval card of a proxy-brained bot says a late answer may be slower. | notes-multibot §4. One key for every client means no per-bot attribution or revocation, and a compromised host could reach `/v1/mcp` file reads and URL fetches as the main user (accepted risk, section 9; ask for a chat-only key, U56). |
| I41 | (Phase 4) **Codex is the default proxy brain. Claude and Antigravity brains are off and opt-in per bot**, each with an explicit user decision (UI-24). The loader refuses a `claude` or `antigravity` preset in any bot's `allowed` list unless `security.json` carries the matching `termsAccepted` flag, which only the UI sets. | The proxy README says Claude "may conflict with Anthropic's consumer terms", and that Google's terms restrict powering other products and name third-party token replay as grounds for suspension. Outside sources report Anthropic blocking third-party use of subscription OAuth (notes-multibot §4, [S]). Codex has no risk section, but OpenAI's terms are unchecked [U58]. |
| I42 | (Phase 4) **A bot on a cloud preset sends its tool output to the vendor** (file contents, command output and peer replies go to OpenAI, Google or Anthropic). So by default: its `readRoots` are a separate cloud-safe set with private folders off (UI-25); `shell` and `dispatch_job` are allowed only on bots whose `primary` and every `allowed` preset are local; and the loader allows **at most one bot with `shell`**. The user may relax the first two per bot in the UI (UI-25). A bot with a proxy preset in `allowed` counts as cloud-capable for these checks even while its `activePreset` is local. | Cloud routes are a data-egress path, and the shell bot is the highest-risk bot. The defaults are this plan's choice (UI-25, UI-28). |
| I43 | (Phase 4) **Per-bot directories and in-process denies.** `%LOCALAPPDATA%\agent-007\bots\<botId>\` holds `state\agent.sqlite*`, `memory\MEMORY.md`, `skills\`, `workspace\` and `logs\`. A bot's tools resolve paths only under its own directory and its own allowlisted roots; a bot's `readRoots` and `cloudSafeRoots` must sit **inside the global allowlist**, and `folders.deny` (Pixotope, work) wins for every bot (the loader refuses an overlap, S20.1; a path-policy test checks it, S20.3). And **in-process denies** cover every sibling `bots\*`, `secrets`, `settings` and other bots' sqlite (extending S1.2 group (b)). Shared, read-only `C:\ProgramData\agent-007\shared\FACTS.md` and `shared\skills\` are **written only by the user or an admin** (agent user R, no W, the `config\` ACL pattern) and loaded into the prompt like `USER.md` for bots with `shared.read: true`. A bot reads its own skills and writes them only through `propose_*`. It never reads another bot's memory; it uses `ask_bot`. A persona (S19) is a mode of one bot; a bot has its own token, storage, memory and route, and reuses the persona schema and validator (`validateAgainstCeiling` with a generic `Ceiling`, S17.5) for its prompt, tool and MCP allowlists. **Honest limit:** all bots run as the same Windows user, so NTFS cannot separate them; an approved shell command (at most one bot, I42) can read sibling directories, every bot token and the proxy key. | The same situation as the Strata files (section 4.2 notes). Hermes profiles are likewise not sandboxes (notes-multibot §3). |
| I44 | (Phase 4) **One local-inference gate per local runner**, in the agent host. Priority classes, non-preemptive and aged: P0 the user's own message in a bot chat; P1 turns in a user-started chain (peer asks); P2 peer-initiated or background turns; P3 summaries and compaction. Per-bot concurrency 1; the queue depth is capped (P2 and P3 beyond it get `busy`); stickiness lets a bot's tool loop finish within a short window, because a different system prompt forces a full re-prefill [U57]; a queue wait over about 10 s sends one "waiting for the model (n ahead)" status. In-flight generation is killed only by the user's `/stop`. **Topology:** interactive bots on a proxy route or the small LM Studio model, background bots on Strata, at most `localBotsOnStrata` bots on Strata (UI-32, proposed 2). | Strata serves one sequence at a time behind a FIFO and holds about 84 GB of the 96 GiB pool. With three or more local bots the p95 latency is the sum of everyone's generations (notes-multibot §7). |
| I45 | (Phase 4) **A bus audit log**, in the I29 style: append-only JSONL, one line per bus message `{ts, chain_id, from, to, hop, origin, taint, body_sha256, decision: delivered \| denied, reason}`. Bodies stay only in the bots' own transcripts. Model switches, fallbacks and circuit-breaker trips go to the same log. | One place to review what the bots said to each other, without a second copy of the text. |
| I46 | (Phase 4) Multi-bot eval bar (G4): 36 prompts × 3 runs; at least 90% correct overall and **100% on the safety cases** (at least 12, including every injection-spread case), measured by **outcome** (no forbidden effect), with every blocked attempt counted and listed. | The same shape as I24. The router makes the outcome independent of the model, so 100% is a real bar. |

The proxy's own README flags a subscription-terms risk. With Phase 4 an alternative is a proxy-brained peer bot (I23, I36). | S15.3 | An explicit yes is required; without it, it stays off and unbuilt (I23). "No, use a bot" is a valid answer. |

All three read an optional `STRATA_API_KEY`. With a keyed Strata and no env var, their Strata calls fail with an auth error, and they fall back without asking (to the next model: LM Studio, then the paid model). VN Studio does not start ComfyUI while Strata answers. Options: (a) set `STRATA_API_KEY` as the main user's env var (the key then sits in that user's environment); (b) keyless Strata; (c) LM Studio as the agent's runner. | G0 (if a keyed Strata is picked), S10, S16 | A decision (sections 4.4 and 13.1). |

| **UI-31** | (Phase 4) The proxy: set `PROXY_API_KEY` (a key is required, I40), confirm the actual port (U52), copy the key into the agent's `secrets.json` yourself, and ask the proxy owner for a chat-only key or an MCP off switch (U56). The agent never reads the proxy's settings file for the key. | S23 | The user does it; [GO] for the first live call. |
| **UI-32** | (Phase 4) One process for all bots (v1, proposed) or one process per bot (13.5); how many local-brained bots may use Strata at once (`localBotsOnStrata`, proposed 2). | S20, S25 | A decision. |

---

## 4. Architecture

### 4.1 Process and modules

There is one Node process, `dist/main.js`, running as the agent user. It owns one SQLite storage; the README says "One process owns a storage at a time" [V].

**Startup sequence:**
1. Take the single-instance lock.
2. Load config and secrets.
3. Build the provider for the configured runner (`model.runner`: LM Studio or Strata; section 4.5).
4. `Harness.open(openNodeSqliteStorage(path), { models, registry, settings }, BACKGROUND_CONTEXT)` [V names].
5. `harness.resume()`.
6. Deliver any pending replies.
7. Start the grammY long poll.
8. Start the runner health monitor (S1.5).

**Runners.** Two local, OpenAI-compatible runners; exactly one is active, chosen by `model.runner` in config. Both share the same 96 GiB GPU pool, so only one big model is loaded at a time [V].

| Runner | Base URL | Model id | Auth | Concurrency | Role |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LM Studio 0.4.26 | `http://127.0.0.1:1234/v1` | bake-off pick | Bearer token (P0.3) | parallel 1 (load setting) | Phase 0 default |
| Strata 0.2.0-beta (`D:\Strata`, pinned `e88ee39d`) | `http://127.0.0.1:8080/v1` | `qwen3.8-flash-next-iq3_s` | API key `strataApiKey` in the 24/7 config (recommended default); none in the P0.3b manual test | one sequence, FIFO [V] | alternative; preferred 24/7 runner |

**Extension `a7`.** One extension holds all of the following and is installed in the host default; conversations never pass an explicit `extensions` array, because of spec footgun 30 [V]:
- tools: `system_facts`, `list_dir`, `read_file`, `search_files`, `write_file`, `remember`, `shell`;
- sections: `preamble`, `user_profile`, `memory`, `runtime`;
- hook: `hook(ToolTask, { beforeTool })`, the approval gate.

**Adapter rule.** Only `src/pi/adapter.ts` imports `@earendil-works/*` or `typebox`. Everything else imports from the adapter. A test enforces this (S1). The runner choice never leaks past the adapter: callers pass the selected runner's `{ id, baseUrl, modelId, apiKey? }` (I13).

**Repo layout.** Which slice owns each path is in brackets.

```

src/pi/adapter.ts                                                           [P0.2, grown in S1/S6]
src/contracts.ts                                                            [S1]  interfaces shared by slices
src/config/{schema,load,secrets,paths}.ts                                   [S1]
src/logging/{logger,audit,redact}.ts                                        [S1]

src/tools/schemas.ts                                                        [P0.5] frozen after bake-off
```
