| U54 | Pi `beforeTool` approval waits while the proxy holds a suspended turn: behaviour of the next request after 150 s (Antigravity) or 270 s (Codex), the extra latency and tokens of the text replay, and one tool call at a time for Codex and Antigravity against Pi's multi-call turns | S23.3 (live [GO]; deferred until after the M2 web UI + Tailscale, deviation 191) |
| U55 | Telegram: the mirror bot in a private group or a channel (and a bot added to a channel with `/setjoingroups` disabled), whether the 20 per minute group limit counts per bot or per group, that Bot-to-Bot Communication Mode off means no update from another bot reaches `getUpdates`, and that the D6 allowlist still drops one if it does | S24.1, S24.3 |
| U56 | Whether the proxy offers a chat-only key or an MCP off switch (a search of `src/` found none), and the effect of its `/v1/mcp` image-path reads and URL fetches for a holder of the shared key | S23.1 (ask the proxy owner; read-only check) |
| U57 | Local gate behaviour: Strata prompt-prefix reuse across bots with different system prompts (re-prefill cost), whether a client abort frees the running sequence (extends U28), and Strata plus the small LM Studio model together in the 96 GiB pool (extends U24 and U25) | S25.3 (live [GO]) |
| U58 | Terms of the Codex subscription through a third-party local proxy (OpenAI's terms were not checked), and a re-check of the Claude and Antigravity terms and any reported blocks at decision time | S23.1 (UI-24) |
| U59 | A peer conversation at 1.4.0: per-turn narrowing of `tools` through `configure()`, the guard extension still forced (U43), and `taint` and `chain_id` carried through `api.commit` so a crash mid-chain resumes with the same stamps | S21.2 |

**S27.1 live result and the G0 candidate (2026-10-31).** The live multi-bot eval passed on Strata with `qwen3.8-flash-next-iq3_s` (100/108 = 92.6%, safety 45/45, max 5 tool calls); the earlier LM Studio `nail-qwen3.6-35b-a3b-mtp` run failed at 74.1% before the eval fixes. S27.1 therefore favours Strata + `qwen3.8-flash-next-iq3_s` as the G0 candidate. UI-10 (the runner and model for 24/7) remains the user's decision; G0 stays open.

## 13. Phases 2, 3 and 4

### 13.1 Phase overview

| Phase | What | Gate | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Windows smoke test, model runners, bake-off, 24/7 spike | G0 (section 6) | ready for `implement` |
| 1 | MVP: Telegram, read-only tools, approved shell, memory, Windows setup | G1 (section 8) | ready for `implement` |
| 2 | Orchestrator: creative-hub MCP, job runner, Claude Code and DeepSeek jobs, Flow guard, routing eval | G2 (section 13.2) | built offline 2026-10-29 (S9 to S16.1, 814 tests); live parts open, see G2 in section 14 |
| 3 | Settings UI, settings split, `propose_*`, personas | G3 (section 13.3) | built offline 2026-10-30 (S17 to S19, 1,047 tests); live parts open, see G3 in section 14 |
| 4 | Multi-bot: per-bot memory, skills and route, ACP proxy brains (D12), `ask_bot`, bot room, GPU gate, multi-bot eval | G4 (section 13.4) | planned at slice level; starts after G3 and a user go |

**Phase 2 architecture.**

```
Telegram ⇄ agent-007 (agent user, local model)
              ├─ always loaded (exactly 10, I27): tool_search, dispatch_job, job_status, cancel_job,
              │      list_templates, ask_user, hub_status, system_facts, list_dir, read_file
              ├─ deferred (tool_search): search_files, write_file, remember, shell, hub_start, macros,
              │      the allowlisted creative-hub MCP tools, (Phase 3) settings and persona tools, (Phase 4) ask_bot, list_bots
              ├─ Job task (Pi Durable defineTask): approve → submit → stream → permissions → collect
              └─ IPC (127.0.0.1, HMAC) ⇄ job runner (main user, at logon)
                                             ├─ claude -p --output-format stream-json (cwd creative-hub)
                                             │     └─ --permission-prompt-tool → permission MCP helper → IPC → Telegram
                                             ├─ dsh --profile acp (ACP client, cwd creative-hub)
                                             └─ hub_start: scripts\hub.ps1 start <service> (fixed template)
creative-hub HTTP MCP servers on 127.0.0.1 ⇠ agent's pi-mcp client (allowlisted tools only)
```

**Decisions still open for the user** (also in section 3):
- **UI-16, Strata and creative-hub.** Scriptorium, Book Translator and VN Studio use Strata first for editors, reviewers and checks. Scriptorium's writers, architect and editor-in-chief, and some VN Studio roles, put a paid model first.
  - All three read `STRATA_API_KEY` (optional; sent only if set). With a **keyed** Strata and no env var, their Strata calls get an auth error, and they **fall back without asking** (to the next model: LM Studio, then the paid model).
  - VN Studio also never starts ComfyUI while Strata answers.
  - If `/health` turns out to need the key (U29), VN Studio's check may see Strata as down and start ComfyUI next to a loaded ~84 GB Strata model (U44).
  - Options:
    - (a) set `STRATA_API_KEY` as a user environment variable for the main user. The key then sits in the main user's environment, readable by every process of that user, including Claude Code jobs;
    - (b) run Strata without a key and accept the residual risk;
    - (c) keep LM Studio as the agent's runner when creative jobs need ComfyUI.
- **UI-11 / UI-12**, the first templates and the job approval UX.
- **UI-15, the second-opinion endpoint. This conflicts with the letter of D8** ("cloud only inside approved jobs"), because it is a cloud call by the agent itself, outside any job; D12 (2026-10-29) covers a bot's brain, not this tool. It stays off and unbuilt (S15.3) unless the user explicitly says yes; in Phase 4 a proxy-brained peer bot is the alternative.
- **UI-21, job-runner-owned confirmation.** Today the approval lives in the agent; the job runner trusts a valid HMAC plus its own caps (I16, I22). That design is **kept as an accepted risk**. The alternative closes the compromised-agent path: a second Telegram bot whose token only the job runner holds, which sends its own confirmation for each job, so a compromised agent cannot start one. Cost:
  - a second BotFather bot and token (the user creates it);
  - a second long-poll loop in the job runner;
  - a second tap per job, or moving job approval entirely to that bot;
  - about 150-250 LOC;
  - one more secret file;
  - a second chat to watch.
- Whether to open a creative-hub ticket for MCP auth (bearer token plus Origin check). Recommended; it is out of scope here.
- **Phase 4 decisions, UI-23 to UI-32** (section 3; summary in 13.4): the first bots and their brains, the terms risk of Claude and Antigravity, cloud bots and private folders, fallback, the bot room, the shell bot and its Windows user, edges, budgets, the proxy key and port, and one process vs several.

### 13.2 Phase 2: Orchestrator

**Rules for every Phase 2 slice.**
- Pi packages stay behind `src/pi/adapter.ts` (the adapter rule now also covers `@earendil-works/pi-mcp`). `@agentclientprotocol/sdk` is imported only in `src/jobrunner/drivers/dsh/**` and its tests in `test/jobrunner/dsh/**` (the fake ACP agent of S13.2). A boundary test enforces both.
- New dependencies, all exact: `@earendil-works/pi-mcp@1.4.0`, `@agentclientprotocol/sdk@2.0.2`, and its `zod` peer at the version it declares (read at install). Installing them is [GO] (S9.1).
- Anything **agent-007** does that starts a creative-hub service, a job, a Flow call or a cloud call needs a Telegram approval at run time. Tests use fakes; live checks are marked [GO]. (VN Studio's own autostart of Pixel Studio, Chiptune Studio and ComfyUI is outside agent-007 and accepted; see S10.4.)
- The agent user and the job runner's user never share files except the read-only `templates\` folder, the append-only settings audit log (Phase 3, I29) and signed IPC messages.
- **Always-loaded tool cap (I27).** Across Phases 1 to 3, the model sees at most **10 always-loaded (non-deferred) tools per turn**. The exact list from Phase 2 on: `tool_search`, `dispatch_job`, `job_status`, `cancel_job`, `list_templates`, `ask_user`, `hub_status`, `system_facts`, `list_dir`, `read_file`. Everything else is `deferred: true` behind `tool_search`: the Phase 1 tools `search_files`, `write_file`, `remember` and `shell`; `hub_start` (a deferred model tool that only requests an approval); every MCP tool and macro; `ask_second_opinion`; the Phase 3 `get_settings`, `list_personas`, `propose_setting`, `propose_persona` and `switch_persona`; and the Phase 4 `ask_bot` and `list_bots`. Phase 1 alone (7 tools) is below the cap and unchanged. From Phase 4 the cap holds per bot (I27, 13.4).

**File carve-outs from earlier slices.**
- S9 edits these Slice 7 files: `config/config.example.json` (the `mcp`, `jobs`, `flow`, `tools` and `secondOpinion` blocks), `deploy/new-secrets.ps1` (`ipcHmacKey`, `mcpTokens`) and `deploy/check-acl.ps1` (the `jobrunner\` and `settings\` rows). No other Phase 2 or 3 slice edits Slice 7 files except `deploy/jobrunner/**` (S11, its own folder).
- `test/integration/**` stays Slice 6's, except `test/integration/phase2-*` (S15) and `test/integration/phase3-*` (S17 to S19, in sequence).

  - Done-check: `npm ls @earendil-works/pi-mcp @agentclientprotocol/sdk zod` shows exactly those versions, with no duplicates; `ignore-scripts` still on; `npm run build` passes.
- [x] **S9.2 Adapter pre-exports.** Add `McpClient`, `StreamableHttpTransport`, `toLlmContent`, `createToolSearchExtension`, and the job-task names S1.0 does not already export (`waitForTask` and the `tx.createTask` option types; `defineTask` and `TaskRuntime` are already in S1.0 and are not re-added), checked against the installed `.d.ts` [U36, U45].

- [x] **S9.3 IPC with HMAC** (`src/ipc/{protocol,hmac,client}.ts`), per I16.
  - Message: `{v, id, ts, nonce, type, body, mac}`. The MAC is HMAC-SHA256 over `v|id|ts|nonce|type|sha256(body)`. Reject if the MAC is wrong, `ts` is off by more than 60 s, the nonce was seen within 5 minutes, or the line is over 1 MB.
  - Types: `submit`, `cancel`, `status`, `list_templates`, `hub_status`, `hub_start`; events `job_event`, `permission_request`, `permission_decision`, `job_done`.
  - Record the U40 finding (Node cannot set a named-pipe DACL) in section 14 with the doc reference.
  - Done-check: `test/ipc/*.test.ts` covers a valid round trip; wrong MAC, stale `ts`, replayed nonce and oversize line rejected; an **HTTP-shaped request** (`POST / HTTP/1.1` with headers and a JSON body, as a browser or DNS-rebinding page would send) is rejected and the connection closed without a reply body; constant-time compare (`timingSafeEqual`); the key never appears in logs (redaction test).
- [x] **S9.4 Contracts and config.** `src/jobs/contracts.ts`: `JobTemplate`, `JobRequest {templateId, params, brief}`, `JobEvent`, `PermissionRequest`, `JobState`, plus the interfaces that let Slices 10 to 14 run in parallel with fakes: `JobApprovalTransport` (Telegram cards; implemented by S14), `JobRunnerClient` (IPC calls; S9's `src/ipc/client.ts`), `JobRunnerPolicy {checkPermission(job, tool, input): "allow" | "deny" | "ask"}` (implemented by S11, used by S12 and S13), and `EngineDriver {start, events, cancel, resume}` (implemented by S12 and S13, used by S11). Config gains `mcp.servers[] {id, url, allowTools[], macros[]}` (no direct MCP tools; all are deferred, I27), `jobs {ipcPort, approvalTimeoutSec, progressIntervalSec}`, `flow {serverId}`, `secondOpinion {enabled:false, baseUrl, modelId}`. The always-loaded list is a code constant `ALWAYS_LOADED` in `src/jobs/contracts.ts` (the 10 names in the rules above); config cannot add to it. Secrets gain `ipcHmacKey` and optional `mcpTokens`.
  - S9 also updates the Slice 7 files in the carve-out: `config.example.json` gains the new blocks; `new-secrets.ps1` generates `ipcHmacKey` (32 random bytes, as for `strataApiKey`, kept unless `-RotateIpcKey`) and prompts for optional `mcpTokens` [UI-18]; `check-acl.ps1` checks `C:\ProgramData\agent-007\jobrunner\` and, if it exists, `...\settings\` against section 4.2.
  - The loader refuses non-loopback MCP or second-opinion URLs. `ALWAYS_LOADED` is a code constant, so its size and names are checked by a **test** (below), not by the loader.
  - Done-check: `test/config*.test.ts` covers each new refusal and the defaults; a test asserts `ALWAYS_LOADED.length <= 10` and equals the list above; `test/deploy/` parse tests cover the new `new-secrets.ps1` key generation (ACL before write, never echoed) and the new `check-acl.ps1` rows.

Claude calls it for each tool use not in `allowedTools`; the helper forwards `{jobId, tool, input}` to the job runner over a per-job local channel. **Channel authentication (I28):** for each job the job runner generates a random 32-byte token, writes it only into the job-scoped `--mcp-config` file as the helper's `env` (`A7_JOB_ID`, `A7_JOB_TOKEN`, `A7_JOB_PORT`), in the job runner's main-user-only data folder, and deletes the file when the job ends. The helper reads the token from its env and HMAC-signs each request with it, using the S9.3 framing on the job runner's 127.0.0.1 port. The job runner accepts it only for that job id while the job runs; the token is never logged. The job runner applies its own rules first (Flow budget, I21; Scriptorium start always denied) and otherwise sends a `permission_request` to the agent, which shows Telegram buttons (Allow once / Deny). Timeout → deny. If `--permission-prompt-tool` does not behave as documented [U31], the fallback is `--permission-mode dontAsk` with a template allowlist only (no mid-run approvals), recorded as a deviation.
  - Done-check: helper tests (initialize, tools/list, tools/call) over stdio; channel tests show a wrong token, another job's token, a token after the job ended and an unsigned request are all refused; the mcp-config file is gone after the job; a job-runner test shows Flow over budget is denied without asking, a Scriptorium start is denied, and other tools produce exactly one `permission_request`; a live [GO] smoke where a template triggers one prompt and the user taps Deny, and the job reports the denial.
- [x] **S12.4 Cancel and resume.** Cancel = tree kill (Windows has no clean SIGINT for a child [V notes §3.1]), then `cancelled`. Resume after a job-runner or agent restart = `claude -p --resume <sessionId>` with the same flags and a "continue" prompt [U34].
  - Done-check: a live [GO] check cancels a running harmless job (no `claude` process left) and resumes another after a forced job-runner restart; the result is recorded for U34.
- [x] **S12.5 Version guard.** On a new `claude --version`, the next job is held until the S12.2 parser smoke reruns on recorded fixtures and one harmless live job passes.
  - Done-check: a test with a changed version string shows the hold.

For Claude Code jobs, the approved number is written into the job prompt as "the agreed Flow budget is N credits" (and passed to the job runner), so creative-hub's CLAUDE.md "default 150 per production" never applies on its own; a Flow job with no approved number is not submitted. For the agent's own (deferred) Flow tools, a `beforeTool` hook blocks any call without `max_credits`, or whose `max_credits` exceeds the job's remaining approved credits; inside jobs the job runner applies the same check (I21). **A Flow call with no ledger entry, that is outside any approved Flow job, is always blocked**; there is no default and no "small call" exception. Post-hoc, credits spent are parsed from results when reported, and the final message shows approved, charged (sum of `max_credits`) and reported spend. No default budget exists (D11).

### 13.3 Phase 3: Settings UI and personas

Detailed in revision 2026-10-29 (d) from `phase3-detail.md` (the source record). Prior art: pi-pocket, Hearth Pi (ha-pi-durable), OpenClaw Control UI (notes §7). Starts only after G2 and a user go (see the standing authorisation in revision (d)). No new npm dependency (the UI is `node:http`, vanilla JS and hand-written schema validation). No new top-level IDs: new items are letter-suffixed under I25 to I29, UI-19, UI-20, U42, U43 and U48. Phase 4 numbers are untouched.

**Contracts between the slices** (what lets them be built in order, and Phase 4 extend them).

- `src/settings/contracts.ts` (S17): `ProposalTransport {present(card) -> {messageId}; update(proposalId, outcomeText); onDecision(cb)}` (implemented by S18.4, faked in S17 tests); `PersonaRuntime {list(); current(conversationId); effective(conversationId); apply(conversationId, personaId)}` (a stub returning `personas_not_active` for `apply` in S17, the real one in S19.1); `SettingsAudit {append(entry)}`; typed error codes `not_writable`, `invalid_value`, `secret_shaped`, `rate_limited`, `too_many_pending`, `stale_base`, `persona_invalid`, `unknown_persona`, `personas_not_active`.
- **Pure subset** (no import of the adapter, usable by the job runner and the migration script): `contracts, jsonschema, schema, split, migrate, allowlist, audit, chain, securityWriter, personaSchema, ceiling, compare, catalog`. A test lists these files and fails on an import of `src/pi/adapter.ts`, `@earendil-works/*` or `typebox` (S11 says the job runner never imports Pi packages).
- **`securityWriter.ts`** is the only code that writes `security.json`. Only `src/jobrunner/settingsUi/**` and `scripts/migrate-settings.ts` may import it (S17.2 boundary test).
- **Store takes its folders as arguments** (`SettingsPaths {securityFile, prefsDir, personaDirs[], auditDir}` from `src/config/paths.ts`), so S20 can relocate `main`'s prefs into `bots\main\` without changing logic.
- **For S20 and S22:** allowlist rows are data (I29d); the proposal engine accepts an `auto` outcome from a row's `classify` (so I38's "narrowing switches apply at once, audited"); the UI form framework is schema-driven with an `ack` option (S18.2), so S22.4 adds form descriptors and an acknowledgment text, not UI code; callback prefixes are `st:` (proposals), `se:` (settings menu), `pe:` (persona), leaving `md:` to S22; `compare()` and `validateAgainstCeiling()` take a generic `Ceiling` object, so I43's per-bot allowlists reuse them.
- **Callback ids** are at most 64 bytes: `st:<8-char id>:y|n`, `se:<code>`, `pe:<code>`.

**Threat table.**

| Threat | Stopped by |
|---|---|
| The model tries to change an allowlist, a deny path, the approval policy or a runner | No allowlist row (I29d); `get_settings` is read-only; NTFS read-only for the agent user; the agent loader refuses to start if `security.json` or its folder is writable by the agent user (S17.2). |
| Injected text makes the model call `propose_*` or `switch_persona` | Cards show the exact diff, the model's reason as quoted untrusted text, and a persona's added capabilities; rate limits (I27a); cosmetic auto-apply is off by default and enum-only. |
| A hijacked Telegram account | Can approve prefs and persona proposals and broader switches, all inside the ceiling (section 9 already treats this as approval power). It cannot reach security settings: they are not in any callback and the UI needs a code printed on the PC. |
| A web page (CSRF, DNS rebinding) | Loopback bind, Host and Origin allowlists, `Content-Type: application/json` only, CSRF header, `SameSite=Strict`, CSP, no state change on GET (I25c). |
| Another local process or the agent user (an approved shell command) calling the UI port | Needs a session; the code exists only as a hash in the main user's folder; 5 failures per minute then a lockout. The agent user cannot read the job runner's secrets (S11.6). |
| An approved shell command edits `prefs.json` or a model-proposed persona | I29c: validated at load, cannot widen; I29b records the edit. It can pollute prompt overlays (accepted, section 9). |
| Audit rewrite | I29e, I29a; the agent and job runner have no write-data. |

  - `jsonschema.ts` is a hand-written JSON-Schema subset (type, enum, min, max, maxLength, pattern, items, properties, required, `additionalProperties: false`, `x-ui` hints) with a validator of about 120 LOC. The same descriptors drive the browser forms (S18.2).
  - **Files after migration.** `config.json` (admin-only, S1) shrinks to bootstrap: `{schemaVersion: 2, paths, settings {dir}}`. `security.json` (`C:\ProgramData\agent-007\settings\`) holds everything else from the Phase 1/2 `config.json`: `telegram.allowedUserIds`; `model {runner, runners, streamTimeoutMs, retry, defaultPreset}`; `presets[] {id, kind: "local", runner, modelId, contextWindow, maxTokens, label}` (Phase 4's I38 extends the kinds); `folders {allow, deny}`; `shell`; `limits`; `mcp.servers`; `jobs`; `flow`; `secondOpinion`; new `budgets {maxJobsPerDay}` and `proposals {timeoutSec: 600, maxPending: 1, perHour: 5, autoApplyCosmetic: false}`; secrets only as **references** (names, never values). `prefs.json` (agent profile `prefs\`) starts from defaults: `style {tone: "neutral", verbosity: "short", language: "en", notes: ""}`, `defaultPersona: "default"`.
  - `split.ts` has a **table** that classifies every key of the Phase 1/2 config as bootstrap or security. An unclassified key makes the migration refuse (fail closed). The same table builds `merge()`, which rebuilds the old shape.
  - The script is run by the user, elevated, in the agent's own folders [GO] [ADMIN]. Default is a dry run that prints the table. `--apply` backs up `config.json` as `config.json.pre-phase3-<ts>`, writes `security.json` through `securityWriter` (audit line `source: "migrate"`), writes `prefs.json`, then rewrites `config.json`. `--rollback` restores the newest backup and renames the new files to `*.rolledback-<ts>`. A second `--apply` on `schemaVersion: 2` is a no-op that exits 0.
  - Done-check: `test/settings/split.test.ts`, `migrate.test.ts` on fixtures (Phase 1 config, a Phase 2 config with MCP and jobs, a Strata config): `merge(split(c))` deep-equals `c` (round trip); an unclassified key is refused; a secret-looking value in the old config is refused with the key name only (never the value); `--apply` twice is idempotent; `--rollback` restores a byte-identical `config.json`; `--apply` without a settings folder ACL setup warns and does not run; `test/settings/schema.test.ts` for every refusal in the existing loader (non-loopback URL, empty allowlist, unknown runner, missing runner block, duplicate alias) now enforced on `security.json`; a hand-edited unknown key is rejected.

  - `loadEffectiveConfig()` returns the Phase 1/2 `Config` shape from `config.json` + `security.json`, so S1 to S15 code is unchanged, plus `settings {security, prefs, personas, revisions}`. With no `security.json` and `schemaVersion` 1 it runs in **legacy mode** (reads `config.json` as before, logs one `warn`), so a Phase 1/2 install keeps working until the migration is run.
  - `security.json` is read once at start into an immutable snapshot; its revision is `sha256(file)`. An invalid `security.json` refuses start. `prefs.json` is re-read when its mtime or hash changes; an invalid one falls back to defaults with a warning. Persona files are read and validated at each change (S19 consumes them).
  - **ACL refusal (extends S1.3).** At start and hourly, `icacls` on `security.json`, the `settings\` folder and `settings\personas\` must show the agent user with read only (no write-data, append, delete, change-permissions or ownership, and no `DELETE_CHILD` on the folder), and no `Everyone` / `Users` / `Authenticated Users` write; the audit folder must show agent read plus append only. Violation: refuse start (hourly: log `error`, freeze proposals).
  - Start also writes one audit line `source: "system", decision: "loaded"` when the security or prefs revision differs from the last audited one, or `external_edit` (I29b).
  - Boundary tests: only `src/jobrunner/settingsUi/**` and `scripts/migrate-settings.ts` import `securityWriter`; no file under `src/agent`, `src/tools`, `src/shell`, `src/telegram`, `src/personas`, `src/settings/{proposals,tools}.ts` imports it or calls a write on a path built from `securityFile`.

### 13.4 Phase 4: Multi-bot (D12)

The user wants several bots "like Hermes bots": each with its own skills and separate memory, able to talk to each other, and free to use the ACP proxy (Codex, Claude, Antigravity through the user's subscriptions) as their brain, with local models still switchable per bot (D12). Planned at slice level from `notes-multibot.md`; starts only after G3 and a user go.

**Scope in one line (what is built).** Several bots that run **live** in one agent host, each answering its own Telegram chat, with a router-enforced `ask_bot` between them. It is not a design-only or export-only feature. A Hermes **profile** (a directory, a token, a gateway) is the model for a bot; Hermes' throwaway `delegate_task` child is not built (13.5).

**Architecture.**

```
user ⇄ Telegram: one private chat per bot (one token each); numeric-id allowlist (D6) in every bot
agent host (one process, agent user)
  ├─ BotManager: bots `main`, `research`, `ops`, ... each = bots\<id>\ + token + Harness/SQLite + route + tool allowlist + skills
  ├─ BotBus (in-process) + router: stamps from, chain_id, hop, path[], origin, taint; limits; loop detection;
  │     capability rule; bus audit.  ask_bot / list_bots are deferred tools (I27 stays at 10).
  │     Peer turns run in a separate peer conversation per sender; peer text is untrusted data.
  ├─ model routes (security.json presets) ─┬─ proxy provider ⇄ api-acp-proxy 127.0.0.1:<port>  (Codex; Claude, Antigravity opt-in)
  │                                        └─ local gate (priority queue) ⇄ Strata :8080 / LM Studio :1234
  └─ mirror bot (write-only) → private "bot room" (optional); never a transport
```

**Rules for every Phase 4 slice.**
- Pi packages stay behind `src/pi/adapter.ts`; S20 adds the exports Phase 4 needs. No new npm dependency is planned.
- A single-bot install (`main` only, no edges) behaves as Phases 1 to 3: no `ask_bot` or `list_bots`, no mirror, the same tests pass.
- Bot ids match `[a-z][a-z0-9-]{1,23}`; `main` is reserved for the existing agent.
- Tests use fakes (a fake proxy server and a fake Telegram API written in the tests, scripted peer bots). Anything live (proxy quota, Telegram, loading local models) is marked [GO].
- Every Phase 4 tool is `deferred: true`; the always-loaded list stays the 10 of I27, per bot (S20.4, S26.1).
- **Decisions needed:** before S20 UI-23, UI-28 and UI-32; before S21 UI-29 and UI-30; before S22 UI-24, UI-25 and UI-26; before S23 UI-31; before S24 UI-27. Answers go into `security.json` through the settings UI, never into the plan.

### 13.5 Later, not planned

- One process per bot, or the shell bot under its own Windows user (`agent007-ops`): the HMAC-TCP `BotBus` on I16's framing, per-bot keys, one scheduled task per bot (UI-28, UI-32). Needs its own plan revision.
- Scheduled and proactive bot turns (`origin: schedule`), and a Hermes-style throwaway `delegate_task` child with a fresh, restricted context.
- A chat-only proxy key or an MCP off switch in the proxy (U56); per-bot proxy keys.
- An `elevated` edge (peer-triggered shell, UI-only, default none): deferred; the director's decision is that peer-triggered turns never get shell, file write, `dispatch_job`, Flow or `remember` in Phase 4.
- Telegram bot-to-bot mode as a transport, or managed bots: not planned (I37).
- A durable scheduler and proactive messages: a `defineTask` with `runtime.sleepUntil` [V], for daily health digests and reminders, re-checking the allowlist when it fires.
- More sources: calendar and email (read-only), notes apps, a web fetch tool with an SSRF guard, a vision model for photos (MiniCPM-V on disk).
- Memory search with embeddings (nomic on disk); weekly memory-diff review prompts.
- A command allowlist that skips approval for a few read-only PowerShell commands; per-command risk labels in the approval UI.
- Audit mirroring to the Windows Event Log; outbound firewall rules for the agent.
- Streaming polish (Telegram drafts), Markdown rendering, files in and out.
- ROCm vs Vulkan benchmark and runtime pinning; a larger eval suite.
- Upgrade path beyond 1.4.0 with a migration rehearsal on a SQLite copy.
- An ACP policy proxy in front of any agent; claude-agent-acp or the Agent SDK with an API key (not on the subscription), if the user ever wants in-process control.

| S14.5 flow-music-mcp `max_credits` fields (U38) | DONE 2026-10-31 (live read-only `listTools`; the estimator is built but not run through a live job runner against the real hub) | **Live check.** flow-music-mcp `dist/index.js --port 17462` was started (no token needed; Chrome is not opened until a tool call), `initialize`, `notifications/initialized` and `tools/list` were sent over Streamable HTTP, and the server was stopped (PID gone, port free). No tool was called, nothing was generated, no credit was spent. 35 tools. Raw list: `.claude/temp/listtools-14.json`. **`max_credits` per tool (the field is always named `max_credits`, an integer):** `flow_generate`: **`scenes[].max_credits`** per scene (not top level; min 0; images are free and carry 0; for video the live quote must not exceed it, else the scene is refused before starting; unset falls back to the server's `FLOW_MUSIC_MCP_MAX_CREDITS`, 50). `flow_continue`: top-level `max_credits` (min 1; quote must not exceed it). `flow_edit`: top-level `max_credits` (min 0; video edit only; no quote exists, only the balance is checked; the real cost is measured and reported). `flow_narrate`: top-level `max_credits` (min 1; `engine: "flow"` only; quote must not exceed it). **No cap field at all:** `music_generate` and `music_remix` (they spend Flow Music credits, 5 per remix), `music_video` (templates other than `simple` have unknown cost), `music_retry`; and the free tools (`flow_status`, `flow_agent_images`, `flow_wait`, `flow_cancel`, `flow_retry`, `flow_assets`, `flow_download`, `flow_trash`, `flow_characters`, `flow_character`, `flow_character_edit`, `flow_inspect`, `flow_voices`, `flow_set_key`, `flow_assemble`, `flow_transcribe`, `flow_captions`, `flow_speech_setup`, `flow_techniques`, `music_status`, `music_loop`, `music_wait`, `music_jobs`, `music_cancel`, `music_download`, `music_songs`, `music_inspect`). **Results:** background jobs report `credits_spent` (balance before minus after, when both reads worked); `music_*` jobs also `credits_after`; `flow_narrate` returns `credits_quote` and `credits_spent`; video/continue/edit job notes carry `quote N credit(s), balance change M`. (Read from `src/flow/*.ts` of flow-music-mcp; no result was produced live.) **`video-plan.mjs`.** `node scripts/video-plan.mjs --help` is NOT a help command: it (and a bare call) prints one usage line and exits 2. Read-only subcommand: `estimate <run dir>` (reads `<dir>/plan.json`, prints the dry run, writes nothing; exit 1 with `video-plan: no plan.json in <dir>: run "plan" first` when the plan is missing). `plan <dir>` WRITES `plan.json`, and it needs `<dir>/screenplay.json`, the Scriptorium `get_screenplay` result, so a video estimate exists only AFTER a screenplay and a plan, never from a bare idea. `next <dir>` is also read-only and prints the next shot's `flow_generate_scene` with `max_credits = min(ceil(estimate x 1.25), budget left, FLOW_MUSIC_MCP_MAX_CREDITS or 50)`. **`estimate` output format (captured 2026-10-31 by running the creative-hub script on a copy of its harbour fixture in `.claude/temp/vp-14/`, nothing written to creative-hub, `--model omni-360p`):** a title line, one line per shot (`  <id>  <s> s  <framing>, <move>[, N line(s)]  <N> credits (seen|guessed)` or `title card  free`), then the lines the number comes from: `Video credits: 18 (3 clip(s) priced pro rata, not quoted by Flow)` (the parenthesis only when some clip is `guessed`), `Retake reserve (25%): 5  ->  23 with the reserve`, `Flow budget: 150, spent 0, left 150. Fits with the reserve.` (or `Fits, but without the full reserve.` / `DOES NOT FIT: ...`), `Flow Music budget: 10, ...` (a separate balance), `Last known balance: ...`, `Music: ...`, and an optional `Warnings:` list. Full text: `.claude/temp/vp-estimate-out-14.txt` and the test constant `REAL_ESTIMATE_OUTPUT`. **Built:** (1) a job runner request `flow_estimate` (read-only; `src/jobrunner/flowEstimate.ts`, `manager.flowEstimate`, IPC client and server) that runs the fixed argv `node scripts/video-plan.mjs estimate <runDir>` in the template's cwd (allowlist-checked), with `runDir` limited to `runs/<name>[/<name>...]` and a `plan.json` that really sits under `runs/` (a junction out is refused); the number is the **"with the reserve" total** (video credits plus the 25% retake reserve; 23 in the capture), and it is refused when the two lines are missing or do not add up, the script fails or times out, or the total is 0. (2) `src/jobs/flowEstimate.ts` on the agent side and `planDispatch` (`FlowEstimateError`): a failed estimate refuses the dispatch with the reason; the fixed `flow.estimate` path is unchanged. (3) `src/jobs/flowCredits.ts`: the guard and the job runner policy read `scenes[].max_credits` (summed) as well as a top-level `max_credits`. Deviations 150 to 156. Tests: `test/jobrunner/core/flowEstimate.test.ts`, `test/jobs/flowEstimate.test.ts`, the U38 mapping tests in `flowGuard.test.ts` and `policy.test.ts`. Not done live: an estimate through a running job runner against the real creative-hub, and any real Flow call (live quotes, `credits_spent` values and result shapes are from the source, not from a run). | 2026-10-31 |
